		<title>Building agentic workflows with SageMaker AI and Bedrock AgentCore</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/building-agentic-workflows-with-sagemaker-ai-and-bedrock-agentcore/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Ayush Sharma]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Fri, 14 Aug 2026 15:58:44 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon SageMaker AI]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">93b011a326051e62ed303da1c918720e315d449d</guid>

					<description>Learn how to combine OpenAI-compatible endpoints on Amazon SageMaker AI with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime to build a multi-agent workflow where each specialized agent uses the model best suited to its job. This post also shows how to get token-level observability from SageMaker endpoints that Strands Agents does not instrument by default.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;A common challenge in building agentic workflows is mixing managed foundation models (FMs) with your own cost-optimized or domain-specific models, without rewriting your agent framework to do it. In this post, we show you how to combine OpenAI-compatible endpoints on Amazon SageMaker AI with Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, and its managed deployment. Specialized agents can collaborate on complex tasks while each uses the model best suited to its job. This combination gives you cost optimization, data residency, and model flexibility in a single production-ready architecture.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We walk through deploying Qwen 3.5 9B on Amazon SageMaker AI, integrating it into a &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents&lt;/a&gt; multi-agent system alongside models on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;, and shipping the entire workflow to Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime. The focus is on the integration mechanics including how to get token-level observability from SageMaker endpoints, which Strands doesn’t provide by default.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The architecture connects three model-hosting paths through a single Amazon Bedrock AgentCore container:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Orchestrator agent (Claude Haiku 4.5 on Bedrock)&lt;/strong&gt; – Classifies user intent and routes tasks through Global cross-Region inference.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Budget agent (Claude Sonnet 4.6 on Bedrock)&lt;/strong&gt; – Handles 50/30/20 budget breakdowns with structured Pydantic output.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Financial analysis agent (Qwen 3.5 9B on Amazon SageMaker AI)&lt;/strong&gt; – Stock analysis and portfolio construction using tool-calling.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock model availability varies by AWS Region. See &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported models by AWS Region in Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;A user request enters the orchestrator agent running inside the Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime. The orchestrator uses the &lt;em&gt;agents as tools&lt;/em&gt; pattern from Strands Agents to route the request to either the budget agent or the financial analysis agent. Both specialized agents call their respective models. The budget agent invokes Claude Sonnet 4.6 through Amazon Bedrock, and the financial analysis agent invokes Qwen 3.5 9B through a SageMaker AI real-time endpoint using the OpenAI-compatible API. Results flow back through the orchestrator to the user. For the complete source code, see the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sagemaker-genai-hosting-examples/tree/main/05-agents/strands/sagemaker-open-ai-compatible-endpoints-agentcore-runtime" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;accompanying GitHub repository&lt;/a&gt;. The following diagram illustrates this architecture.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div id="attachment_137249" style="width: 1043px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-137249" loading="lazy" class="wp-image-137249 size-full" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/14/Blog.drawioUpdated_v1.png" alt="" width="1033" height="491"&gt;
 &lt;p id="caption-attachment-137249" class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Architecture of the multi-agent workflow across Amazon Bedrock and Amazon SageMaker AI&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You must have the following prerequisites to follow along with this post.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AWS account with permissions for Amazon SageMaker AI, Amazon Bedrock, and AgentCore.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p style="padding-left: 40px"&gt;&lt;code&gt;pip install sagemaker-core openai httpx strands-agents[otel] yfinance pydantic bedrock-agentcore&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) role with &lt;code&gt;sagemaker:InvokeEndpoint&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;sagemaker:CallWithBearerToken&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Bedrock model access for Claude Haiku 4.5 and Claude Sonnet 4.6.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Python 3.12+.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-1-deploy-qwen-3.5-9b-on-sagemaker-ai"&gt;Step 1: Deploy Qwen 3.5 9B on SageMaker AI&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Deploy Qwen 3.5 9B using the vLLM Deep Learning Container (DLC), image &lt;code&gt;vllm:0.22.1-gpu-py312-cu130&lt;/code&gt;, on &lt;code&gt;ml.g6e.2xlarge&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;region = "us-west-2"
model_id = "Qwen/Qwen3.5-9B"
instance_type = "ml.g6e.2xlarge"  # 1x L40S (48GB VRAM)
num_gpu = 1

# vLLM 0.22.1, Python 3.12, CUDA 13.0, Ubuntu 22.04
inference_image = f"763104351884.dkr.ecr.{region}.amazonaws.com/vllm:0.22.1-gpu-py312-cu130-ubuntu22.04-sagemaker"

env = {
    "SM_VLLM_MODEL": model_id,
    "SM_VLLM_TENSOR_PARALLEL_SIZE": "1",
    "SM_VLLM_MAX_MODEL_LEN": "32768",
}

# Create Model
sm.create_model(
    ModelName=model_name,
    ExecutionRoleArn=role,
    PrimaryContainer={"Image": inference_image, "Environment": env},
)

# Create Endpoint Config + Endpoint
sm.create_endpoint_config(
    EndpointConfigName=endpoint_config_name,
    ProductionVariants=[{
        "VariantName": "v1",
        "ModelName": model_name,
        "InstanceType": instance_type,
        "InitialInstanceCount": 1,
        "ContainerStartupHealthCheckTimeoutInSeconds": 1200,
        "InferenceAmiVersion": inference_ami_version,
    }],
)

sm.create_endpoint(EndpointName=endpoint_name, EndpointConfigName=endpoint_config_name)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-2-build-the-multi-agent-system"&gt;Step 2: Build the multi-agent system&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The OpenAI-compatible API of SageMaker AI expects a bearer token. Tokens expire, so for any long-running agent session you need a way to refresh them on every request. Set up auto-refreshing bearer tokens with an &lt;code&gt;httpx.Auth&lt;/code&gt; subclass:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;import httpx
from openai import AsyncOpenAI
from sagemaker.core.token_generator import generate_token

class SageMakerAuth(httpx.Auth):
    def __init__(self, region): self.region = region
    def auth_flow(self, request):
        request.headers["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {generate_token(region=self.region)}"
        yield request

strands_client = AsyncOpenAI(
    base_url=f"https://runtime.sagemaker.{REGION}.amazonaws.com/endpoints/{ENDPOINT_NAME}/openai/v1",
    api_key="sagemaker",
    http_client=httpx.AsyncClient(auth=SageMakerAuth(region=REGION)),
)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Build using Strands Agents’ &lt;em&gt;agents as tools&lt;/em&gt; pattern with fresh agent instances per invocation.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from strands import Agent, tool
from strands.models.openai import OpenAIModel

qwen_model = OpenAIModel(
    client=strands_client, model_id="",
    params={"temperature": 0.7, "max_tokens": 4096, "stream_options": {"include_usage": True}},
)

@tool
def financial_analysis_agent_tool(query: str) -&amp;gt; str:
    fresh = Agent(model=qwen_model, tools=[...], callback_handler=None)
    return str(fresh(query))

orchestrator = Agent(
    model=BedrockModel(model_id="global.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0"),
    tools=[budget_agent_tool, financial_analysis_agent_tool],
)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="step-3-deploy-to-amazon-bedrock-agentcore-runtime"&gt;Step 3: Deploy to Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Deploy using the &lt;code&gt;bedrock-agentcore-starter-toolkit&lt;/code&gt;. See &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sagemaker-genai-hosting-examples/blob/main/05-agents/strands/sagemaker-open-ai-compatible-endpoints-agentcore-runtime/Lab3/deploy_agentcore.ipynb" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;deploy_agentcore.ipynb&lt;/a&gt; for the full deployment notebook.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from bedrock_agentcore_starter_toolkit import Runtime

agentcore_runtime = Runtime()
agentcore_runtime.configure(
    entrypoint="main.py", auto_create_execution_role=True,
    auto_create_ecr=True, requirements_file="requirements.txt",
    region="ap-south-1", agent_name="personal_finance_agent",
)

launch_result = agentcore_runtime.launch(
    env_vars={
        "SAGEMAKER_ENDPOINT_NAME": "qwen35-9b-260612-082732",
        "SAGEMAKER_REGION": "ap-south-1",
        "AGENT_OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED": "true",
    }
)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="configure-observability-for-sagemaker-endpoints"&gt;Configure observability for SageMaker endpoints&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime instruments your agents with OpenTelemetry automatically, but that instrumentation doesn’t extend equally to every model provider. Before you can monitor cost and latency for the Qwen model on Amazon SageMaker AI, you must understand where the default instrumentation falls short and how to close that gap.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="the-challenge-invisible-token-usage"&gt;The challenge: Invisible token usage&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime automatically instruments agents using OpenTelemetry. However, there is a critical gap:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Amazon Bedrock model calls&lt;/strong&gt; get full generative AI spans with token counts automatically. No extra work is needed.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Amazon SageMaker OpenAI-compatible endpoints&lt;/strong&gt; (through Strands &lt;code&gt;OpenAIModel&lt;/code&gt;) don’t get automatic token telemetry. The instrumentation doesn’t recognize them as generative AI calls.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This means tokens consumed by the financial analysis agent calling Qwen 3.5 9B on Amazon SageMaker are completely invisible in traces. You cannot monitor cost, detect regressions, or debug latency.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Root cause:&lt;/strong&gt; Strands’ OTEL integration emits spans for tool calls and agent lifecycle events, but it doesn’t emit &lt;code&gt;gen_ai.chat&lt;/code&gt; spans with token attributes for the &lt;code&gt;OpenAIModel&lt;/code&gt; provider. The auto-instrumentation of AgentCore only recognizes Amazon Bedrock model inference calls (made through &lt;code&gt;boto3&lt;/code&gt;) as generative AI operations.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="the-solution-custom-opentelemetry-spans"&gt;The solution: Custom OpenTelemetry spans&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Manually emit a &lt;code&gt;gen_ai.chat&lt;/code&gt; span that wraps the Amazon SageMaker agent invocation and extracts token usage from Strands’ internal &lt;code&gt;AgentResult.metrics.accumulated_usage&lt;/code&gt;:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from opentelemetry import trace

tracer = trace.get_tracer("financial_analysis_agent")

@tool
def financial_analysis_agent_tool(query: str) -&amp;gt; str:
    """Route investment queries to Qwen on SageMaker with observability."""
    with tracer.start_as_current_span("gen_ai.chat", attributes={
        "gen_ai.system": "openai",
        "gen_ai.request.model": f"qwen3.5-9b ({SAGEMAKER_ENDPOINT_NAME})",
        "gen_ai.operation.name": "chat",
    }) as span:
        fa_agent = Agent(
            model=OpenAIModel(
                client=strands_client, model_id="",
                params={"temperature": 0.7, "max_tokens": 4096,
                        "stream_options": {"include_usage": True}},
            ),
            system_prompt=FINANCIAL_ANALYSIS_PROMPT,
            tools=[get_stock_analysis, create_diversified_portfolio, compare_stock_performance],
            callback_handler=None,
        )
        result = fa_agent(query)
        # Extract token usage from Strands agent metrics
        usage = result.metrics.accumulated_usage
        span.set_attribute("gen_ai.usage.input_tokens", usage.get("inputTokens", 0))
        span.set_attribute("gen_ai.usage.output_tokens", usage.get("outputTokens", 0))
        span.set_attribute("gen_ai.usage.total_tokens", usage.get("totalTokens", 0))
        return str(result)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Key detail:&lt;/strong&gt; Strands tracks token usage internally with keys &lt;code&gt;inputTokens&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;outputTokens&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;totalTokens&lt;/code&gt;. This dict is populated only if the model provider returns usage data.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="why-stream_options-is-mandatory-for-vllm"&gt;Why stream_options is mandatory for vLLM&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;By default, vLLM doesn’t include a usage chunk in streaming responses. Strands receives text chunks but never a final usage object. As a result, &lt;code&gt;accumulated_usage&lt;/code&gt; stays at zero. Adding &lt;code&gt;stream_options: {"include_usage": True}&lt;/code&gt; tells vLLM to send an extra final chunk with token counts:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;qwen_model = OpenAIModel(
    client=strands_client,
    model_id="",
    params={
        "temperature": 0.7,
        "max_tokens": 4096,
        "stream_options": {"include_usage": True},  # Critical for token tracking
    },
)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Without this parameter, your &lt;code&gt;gen_ai.chat&lt;/code&gt; spans report 0 tokens. This defeats the purpose of the custom span.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-by-step-configuration"&gt;Step-by-step configuration&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Turn on Amazon CloudWatch Transaction Search&lt;/strong&gt; (one-time per account or Region): 
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws xray update-trace-segment-destination --region ap-south-1 --destination CloudWatchLogs

aws xray update-indexing-rule --region ap-south-1 --name "Default" \
  --rule '{"Probabilistic": {"DesiredSamplingPercentage": 100}}'&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
  &lt;/div&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Install Strands with OTEL extras:&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;code&gt;strands-agents[otel]&amp;gt;=1.0.0&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Set&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;code&gt;AGENT_OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED=true&lt;/code&gt; in your code or env vars.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;code&gt;opentelemetry-instrument&lt;/code&gt; as the container CMD.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Add&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;code&gt;stream_options: {"include_usage": True}&lt;/code&gt; to &lt;code&gt;OpenAIModel&lt;/code&gt; params.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Create custom&lt;/strong&gt; &lt;code&gt;gen_ai.chat&lt;/code&gt; span wrapping the SageMaker agent call.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="example-trace-output"&gt;Example trace output&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
    "name": "gen_ai.chat",
    "attributes": {
        "gen_ai.system": "openai",
        "gen_ai.request.model": "qwen3.5-9b (qwen35-9b-260612-082732)",
        "gen_ai.operation.name": "chat",
        "gen_ai.usage.input_tokens": 1391,
        "gen_ai.usage.output_tokens": 1432,
        "gen_ai.usage.total_tokens": 2823
    },
    "durationNano": 37237386894
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="agent-trajectory-on-bedrock-agentcore-observability-dashboard"&gt;Agent trajectory on Bedrock AgentCore Observability dashboard&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This trace view shows the &lt;code&gt;gen_ai.chat&lt;/code&gt; span for the Amazon SageMaker AI hosted Qwen model alongside the automatically instrumented Amazon Bedrock AgentCore spans, with token counts now visible for both. Building this end-to-end observability surfaced several implementation details worth calling out.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/10/ML-21454-2-1.png" alt="AgentCore observability dashboard trace showing the gen_ai.chat span with input, output, and total token counts for the SageMaker-hosted Qwen model" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 2: AgentCore observability trace with token counts for the SageMaker-hosted model&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="key-learnings"&gt;Key learnings&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore auto-instruments Bedrock calls&lt;/strong&gt; – No extra work for Claude or Amazon Nova.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;SageMaker OpenAI endpoints need manual spans&lt;/strong&gt; – Strands doesn’t emit &lt;code&gt;gen_ai.chat&lt;/code&gt; spans for &lt;code&gt;OpenAIModel&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Token usage requires stream_options&lt;/strong&gt; – vLLM doesn’t send usage in streaming by default.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use result.metrics.accumulated_usage&lt;/strong&gt; – Keys: &lt;code&gt;inputTokens&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;outputTokens&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;totalTokens&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AWS X-Ray sampling rate matters&lt;/strong&gt; – Default 1 percent drops most traces. Use 100 percent during development.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Fresh agent instances per request&lt;/strong&gt; – Singletons cause concurrent invocation errors.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="extending-the-pattern"&gt;Extending the pattern&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This architecture is composable. A few directions to explore:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Swap in fine-tuned models&lt;/strong&gt;: Point &lt;code&gt;SM_VLLM_MODEL&lt;/code&gt; to your fine-tuned checkpoint on Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3). The auth layer, OTEL spans, and AgentCore deployment stay unchanged.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;A/B test with inference components:&lt;/strong&gt; Deploy base and fine-tuned variants on the same Amazon SageMaker endpoint. Add a variant attribute to your OTEL span to compare quality in traces.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Cost-aware routing:&lt;/strong&gt; Check query complexity before dispatch. Route simple lookups to Haiku on Amazon Bedrock. Reserve the Amazon SageMaker GPU endpoint for multi-step reasoning tasks.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="cleaning-up"&gt;Cleaning up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To avoid incurring future charges, delete the resources:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;agentcore_control = boto3.client("bedrock-agentcore-control", region_name=region)
agentcore_control.delete_agent_runtime(agentRuntimeId=launch_result.agent_id)
sagemaker_client.delete_endpoint(EndpointName=ENDPOINT_NAME)
sagemaker_client.delete_endpoint_config(EndpointConfigName=f"qwen35-9b-epc-{TIMESTAMP}")
sagemaker_client.delete_model(ModelName=f"qwen35-9b-{TIMESTAMP}")&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we showed how to connect a self-hosted model on Amazon SageMaker AI to Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime, and critically, how to get full token-level observability from Amazon SageMaker endpoints that Strands Agents doesn’t instrument by default.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;httpx.Auth&lt;/code&gt; + &lt;code&gt;generate_token()&lt;/code&gt; + &lt;code&gt;AsyncOpenAI&lt;/code&gt; – Production-ready SageMaker authentication inside AgentCore.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Custom &lt;code&gt;gen_ai.chat&lt;/code&gt; OTEL span + &lt;code&gt;stream_options: {"include_usage": True}&lt;/code&gt; – Full token visibility for Amazon SageMaker endpoints.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;result.metrics.accumulated_usage&lt;/code&gt; – The Strands API for extracting token counts.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started, clone the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sagemaker-genai-hosting-examples/tree/main/05-agents/strands/sagemaker-open-ai-compatible-endpoints-agentcore-runtime" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;accompanying repository&lt;/a&gt; and see &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sagemaker-genai-hosting-examples/blob/main/05-agents/strands/sagemaker-open-ai-compatible-endpoints-agentcore-runtime/Lab3/OBSERVABILITY.md" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;OBSERVABILITY.md&lt;/a&gt; for the complete reference.&lt;/p&gt;

		<title>Monitor on-premises and multi-cloud AI agents with AgentCore Observability</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/monitor-on-premises-and-multi-cloud-ai-agents-with-agentcore-observability/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Vipul Rajendra Gargav]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Thu, 13 Aug 2026 16:02:10 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock AgentCore]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">b5d7724d73cc7030cb1cb40507aa91d081f551a4</guid>

					<description>Set up Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Observability for AI agents running outside AWS: on-premises, on GCP, on Azure, or on developer machines. This walkthrough uses the AWS Distro for OpenTelemetry (ADOT) and IAM credentials to route session traces, span metrics, and token usage to the same AgentCore Observability dashboard.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;When you deploy AI agents built with frameworks like Strands Agents, LangGraph, and CrewAI, you need observability into their performance. This holds true whether they run on Amazon Elastic Kubernetes Service (Amazon EKS), Amazon Elastic Container Service (Amazon ECS), AWS Lambda, on-premises, or another cloud provider such as Google Cloud Platform (GCP) or Microsoft Azure.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/what-is-bedrock-agentcore.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt; is a platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale, with any framework or model. Although &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/observability.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Observability&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, provides native tracing, monitoring, and analytics that local cloud monitoring tools don’t offer out of the box, it natively supports only agents deployed on AgentCore runtime in the AWS Cloud. If your agents run anywhere else, you need additional configuration to send telemetry to the dashboard.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we show you how to set up observability for agents running outside AWS. You learn how to configure the AWS Distro for OpenTelemetry (ADOT) auto-instrumentation in non-AWS environments, route telemetry to the AgentCore Observability dashboard, and validate the setup end to end.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram shows the end-to-end observability pipeline and how telemetry flows from agents to the AgentCore Observability dashboard.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/10/ML-20910-1.png" alt="End-to-end observability pipeline showing telemetry flowing from agents outside AWS to the AgentCore Observability dashboard" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 1: End-to-end observability pipeline from agents to the AgentCore Observability dashboard&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The solution uses the &lt;a href="https://aws-otel.github.io/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Distro for OpenTelemetry (ADOT)&lt;/a&gt; running in-process with the agent application. ADOT auto-instruments the agent framework and captures generative AI semantic convention spans, then exports the telemetry directly to the Amazon CloudWatch OpenTelemetry Protocol (OTLP) endpoint using SigV4 authentication with AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) credentials.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Sending telemetry from your AI agent to Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Observability requires three core components:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;ADOT auto-instrumentation&lt;/strong&gt;: The AWS Distro for OpenTelemetry handles the complexities of exporting telemetry from non-AWS environments.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;IAM credentials&lt;/strong&gt;: The ADOT uses these access keys to authenticate with CloudWatch and forward your agent’s telemetry (traces, metrics, and logs) to the AgentCore Observability dashboard.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Environment variables&lt;/strong&gt;: These contain specific OpenTelemetry settings related to routing and authentication.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;As seen in the following diagram, this cross-platform observability solution integrates several AWS services. Amazon CloudWatch serves as the foundation, handling telemetry ingestion and storage. Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Observability adds specialized monitoring dashboards for AI agents. AWS Distro for OpenTelemetry (ADOT) provides the cross-platform instrumentation capabilities. IAM secures the authentication between your external environments and AWS.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/10/ML-20910-2.jpeg" alt="Cross-platform observability architecture integrating Amazon CloudWatch, Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Observability, ADOT, and AWS IAM" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 2: Cross-platform observability architecture and the AWS services involved&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Observability is a foundational pillar of responsible AI. By routing telemetry to AgentCore Observability, you gain visibility into agent reasoning chains, tool invocations, and model outputs. This allows you to detect hallucinations, monitor for harmful or off-topic responses, track token usage for cost governance, and audit agent behavior across environments. This is especially critical for agents running outside AWS, where problematic outputs might go unnoticed without centralized observability.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Before you begin, verify that you have:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;An AWS account: 
  &lt;ul&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;with Amazon Bedrock model access configured (this walkthrough uses &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/model-card-anthropic-claude-haiku-4-5.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Claude Haiku&lt;/a&gt;). For model availability by AWS Region, refer to &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-region-compatibility.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;supported models by AWS Region&lt;/a&gt; in Amazon Bedrock.&lt;/li&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;for designated AgentCore Observability and designated log group(s).&lt;/li&gt; 
  &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudWatch/latest/monitoring/Enable-TransactionSearch.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;CloudWatch Transaction Search&lt;/a&gt; turned on in your account (one-time setup)&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Python 3.10 or later installed on your non-AWS environment.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;IAM user credentials (access key ID and secret access key) with permissions for: 
  &lt;ul&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;bedrock:InvokeModel&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;logs:CreateLogGroup&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;logs:CreateLogStream&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;logs:PutLogEvents&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;xray:PutTraceSegments&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;xray:PutTelemetryRecords&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;xray:GetSamplingRules&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;xray:GetSamplingTargets&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;cloudwatch:PutMetricData&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
  &lt;/ul&gt; &lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Outbound HTTPS access to AWS endpoints from your environment.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="turn-on-cloudwatch-transaction-search"&gt;Turn on CloudWatch Transaction Search&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;If you haven’t turned on Transaction Search, run the following (one-time per account):&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws xray update-trace-segment-destination --destination CloudWatchLogs --region us-east-1&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Verify it’s active:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;aws xray get-trace-segment-destination --region us-east-1
# Expected: {"Destination": "CloudWatchLogs", "Status": "ACTIVE"}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="how-it-works"&gt;How it works&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The ADOT auto-instrumentation (&lt;code&gt;aws-opentelemetry-distro&lt;/code&gt;) handles the complexity of exporting telemetry from non-AWS environments to CloudWatch:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Auto-instrumentation&lt;/strong&gt;: The &lt;code&gt;opentelemetry-instrument&lt;/code&gt; command injects the ADOT into the Python runtime. It automatically patches &lt;code&gt;boto3&lt;/code&gt; (for Amazon Bedrock calls) and the Strands framework (for agent reasoning spans) to emit OpenTelemetry traces.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;SigV4 authentication&lt;/strong&gt;: The &lt;code&gt;aws_configurator&lt;/code&gt; uses the &lt;code&gt;boto3&lt;/code&gt; credential chain to sign OTLP export requests with SigV4. From non-AWS environments, this uses the &lt;code&gt;AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY&lt;/code&gt; environment variables.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;CloudWatch OTLP endpoint&lt;/strong&gt;: The ADOT exports traces and logs to the CloudWatch native OTLP ingestion endpoint. The &lt;code&gt;OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_HEADERS&lt;/code&gt; header directs logs to the specific AgentCore log group, which is how CloudWatch indexes the data under the generative AI observability dashboard. For details on how the CloudWatch OTLP endpoint URL is determined and configured, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudWatch/latest/monitoring/CloudWatch-OTLPEndpoint.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;CloudWatch OTLP endpoint&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Generative AI semantic conventions&lt;/strong&gt;: The Strands &lt;code&gt;[otel]&lt;/code&gt; package emits spans following the &lt;a href="https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/semconv/gen-ai/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;OpenTelemetry generative AI semantic conventions&lt;/a&gt;, including agent reasoning steps, tool invocations, and model calls with token usage.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram shows how telemetry export through ADOT auto-instrumentation works from non-AWS environments to CloudWatch.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/10/ML-20910-3.jpg" alt="Telemetry export flow through ADOT auto-instrumentation from non-AWS environments to Amazon CloudWatch" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 3: Telemetry export through ADOT auto-instrumentation from non-AWS environments to CloudWatch&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="walkthrough"&gt;Walkthrough&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Follow these steps to configure and run a Strands agent in a non-AWS environment, with telemetry routed to AgentCore Observability.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-1-install-dependencies"&gt;Step 1: Install dependencies&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;On your non-AWS environment (on-premises server, GCP VM, Azure VM, or a compute with internet access):&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;pip install "aws-opentelemetry-distro&amp;gt;=0.10.0" boto3 "strands-agents[otel]"&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;code&gt;aws-opentelemetry-distro&lt;/code&gt; package includes the ADOT auto-instrumentation with OTLP exporters specific to AWS and the &lt;code&gt;aws_configurator&lt;/code&gt; that handles SigV4 authentication. The &lt;code&gt;strands-agents[otel]&lt;/code&gt; package provides OpenTelemetry trace emission from the Strands framework.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-2-configure-aws-credentials"&gt;Step 2: Configure AWS credentials&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Set your IAM user credentials as environment variables.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;export AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID=&amp;lt;your-access-key-id&amp;gt;
export AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=&amp;lt;your-secret-access-key&amp;gt;
export AWS_REGION=us-east-1&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Security note&lt;/strong&gt;: For production deployments, consider using &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/rolesanywhere/latest/userguide/introduction.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;IAM Roles Anywhere&lt;/a&gt; instead of long-lived access keys. With IAM Roles Anywhere, on-premises workloads can obtain temporary credentials using X.509 certificates.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-3-set-opentelemetry-environment-variables"&gt;Step 3: Set OpenTelemetry environment variables&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;These environment variables configure the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/observability-get-started.html#enabling-observability-non-runtime-hosted" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;ADOT to route telemetry to the AgentCore Observability&lt;/a&gt; dashboard:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;export AGENT_OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED=true
export OTEL_PYTHON_DISTRO=aws_distro
export OTEL_PYTHON_CONFIGURATOR=aws_configurator
export OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES="service.name=my-external-agent,aws.log.group.names=/aws/bedrock-agentcore/runtimes/my-external-agent"
export OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_HEADERS="x-aws-log-group=/aws/bedrock-agentcore/runtimes/my-external-agent,x-aws-log-stream=runtime-logs,x-aws-metric-namespace=bedrock-agentcore"
export OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL=http/protobuf
export OTEL_TRACES_EXPORTER=otlp&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Key configuration details:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;AGENT_OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED=true&lt;/code&gt; activates generative AI-specific telemetry processing in the ADOT.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;OTEL_PYTHON_DISTRO=aws_distro&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;OTEL_PYTHON_CONFIGURATOR=aws_configurator&lt;/code&gt; activate the OpenTelemetry configuration specific to AWS, including SigV4 signing for the CloudWatch OTLP endpoint.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES&lt;/code&gt; with &lt;code&gt;aws.log.group.names&lt;/code&gt; tells CloudWatch to index the telemetry under the AgentCore Observability dashboard. Without this, traces go to generic Amazon CloudWatch Logs.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_HEADERS&lt;/code&gt; with &lt;code&gt;x-aws-metric-namespace=bedrock-agentcore&lt;/code&gt; routes metrics in &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonCloudWatch/latest/monitoring/CloudWatch_Embedded_Metric_Format.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;embedded metric format&lt;/a&gt; to the correct CloudWatch namespace.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-4-create-the-agent-application"&gt;Step 4: Create the agent application&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Create a file named &lt;code&gt;agent_test.py&lt;/code&gt; with a Strands agent:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from strands import Agent
from strands.models.bedrock import BedrockModel
from opentelemetry import baggage
from opentelemetry.context import attach
import time

# Configure the Bedrock model
model = BedrockModel(
    model_id="us.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0",
    region_name="us-east-1"
)
# Create the agent
agent = Agent(
    model=model,
    system_prompt="You are a helpful travel assistant."
)

# Set session ID for AgentCore session tracking
# All agent calls after attach() share same session ID for multiple requests/responses
session_id = f"external-session-{int(time.time())}"
ctx = baggage.set_baggage("session.id", session_id)
attach(ctx)

# Run the agent
response = agent("What are the top 3 things to do in Tokyo?")
print(response)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-5-run-with-adot-auto-instrumentation"&gt;Step 5: Run with ADOT auto-instrumentation&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The &lt;code&gt;opentelemetry-instrument&lt;/code&gt; command wraps your Python process with the ADOT, automatically instrumenting Amazon Bedrock calls and Strands framework operations:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;opentelemetry-instrument python3.12 agent_test.py&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The agent’s response appears in the terminal. Behind the scenes, the ADOT captures traces, spans, and logs, and exports them to CloudWatch.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="step-6-verify-in-agentcore-observability"&gt;Step 6: Verify in AgentCore Observability&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;You see telemetry data within two to three minutes of execution. Open the Amazon CloudWatch console:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose &lt;strong&gt;GenAI Observability&lt;/strong&gt;, then &lt;strong&gt;Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/strong&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;In the &lt;strong&gt;Agents&lt;/strong&gt; tab, look for &lt;code&gt;my-external-agent&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;Choose the agent to view sessions, traces, and span metrics.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following screenshot shows the telemetry from the Strands agent (&lt;code&gt;my-external-agent&lt;/code&gt;) running in a non-AWS environment, as seen in the AgentCore Observability dashboard in CloudWatch.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/10/ML-20910-4.jpg" alt="AgentCore Observability dashboard in CloudWatch showing the my-external-agent with its sessions and traces" width="800"&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;&lt;br&gt;Figure 4: The my-external-agent telemetry in the AgentCore Observability dashboard&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The console shows:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ul&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Agent name&lt;/strong&gt;: &lt;code&gt;my-external-agent&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Sessions&lt;/strong&gt;: at least one session.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Traces&lt;/strong&gt;: trace spans showing the agent’s reasoning and Amazon Bedrock model invocations.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Span details&lt;/strong&gt;: &lt;code&gt;invoke_agent&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;chat&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;execute_event_loop_cycle&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;chat.us.anthropic.claude-haiku&lt;/code&gt; spans with latency and token metrics.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ul&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following screenshot shows a successful trace from the Strands agent (&lt;code&gt;my-external-agent&lt;/code&gt;) with four spans, model information, and latency and token details in the AgentCore Observability dashboard.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div id="attachment_137138" style="width: 2258px" class="wp-caption alignleft"&gt;
 &lt;img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-137138" loading="lazy" class="size-full wp-image-137138" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/12/ml-20910-image5.png" alt="" width="2248" height="1380"&gt;
 &lt;p id="caption-attachment-137138" class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 5: Trace detail for my-external-agent with span, latency, and token metrics&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="validating-from-google-cloud-platform"&gt;Validating from Google Cloud Platform&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To confirm the solution works from a third-party cloud provider, we tested the same setup from Google Cloud Shell, a browser-based terminal running on GCP infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Set up the environment on Google Cloud Shell:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;# Create a virtual environment
python3.12 -m venv venv
source venv/bin/activate
# Install dependencies
pip install "aws-opentelemetry-distro" boto3 "strands-agents[otel]"
# Set AWS credentials
export AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID=&amp;lt;your-access-key-id&amp;gt;
export AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY=&amp;lt;your-secret-access-key&amp;gt;
export AWS_REGION=us-east-1
# Set ADOT environment variables
export AGENT_OBSERVABILITY_ENABLED=true
export OTEL_PYTHON_DISTRO=aws_distro
export OTEL_PYTHON_CONFIGURATOR=aws_configurator
export OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES="service.name=gcp-hosted-agent,aws.log.group.names=/aws/bedrock-agentcore/runtimes/gcp-hosted-agent"
export OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_LOGS_HEADERS="x-aws-log-group=/aws/bedrock-agentcore/runtimes/gcp-hosted-agent,x-aws-log-stream=runtime-logs,x-aws-metric-namespace=bedrock-agentcore"
export OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_PROTOCOL=http/protobuf
export OTEL_TRACES_EXPORTER=otlp&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Run the agent from GCP:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;cat &amp;gt; agent_test.py &amp;lt;&amp;lt; 'EOF'
from strands import Agent
from strands.models.bedrock import BedrockModel
from opentelemetry import baggage
from opentelemetry.context import attach
import time

model = BedrockModel(
    model_id="us.anthropic.claude-haiku-4-5-20251001-v1:0",
    region_name="us-east-1"
)
agent = Agent(model=model, system_prompt="You are a helpful assistant.")

# Set session ID for AgentCore session tracking
# All agent calls after attach() share same session ID for multiple requests/responses
session_id = f"gcp-session-{int(time.time())}"
ctx = baggage.set_baggage("session.id", session_id)
attach(ctx)

response = agent("What are the top 3 things to do in Paris?")
print(response)
EOF
opentelemetry-instrument python3.12 agent_test.py&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following screenshot shows the Strands agent (&lt;code&gt;gcp-hosted-agent&lt;/code&gt;) running on Google Cloud Shell (GCP) and returning a successful response.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div id="attachment_137139" style="width: 2254px" class="wp-caption alignleft"&gt;
 &lt;img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-137139" loading="lazy" class="size-full wp-image-137139" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/12/ml-20910-image6.png" alt="" width="2244" height="1388"&gt;
 &lt;p id="caption-attachment-137139" class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 6: The gcp-hosted-agent running in Google Cloud Shell&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;h3 id="verify-cross-cloud-telemetry"&gt;Verify cross-cloud telemetry&lt;/h3&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Within two to three minutes of execution, the &lt;code&gt;gcp-hosted-agent&lt;/code&gt; appears in the AgentCore Observability dashboard alongside agents running on AgentCore runtime or other environments.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The following screenshot shows a successful trace from the Strands agent (&lt;code&gt;gcp-hosted-agent&lt;/code&gt;) running on GCP with four spans, model information, and latency and token details in the AgentCore Observability dashboard.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div id="attachment_137140" style="width: 2912px" class="wp-caption alignleft"&gt;
 &lt;img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-137140" loading="lazy" class="size-full wp-image-137140" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/08/12/ml-20910-image7.png" alt="" width="2902" height="1366"&gt;
 &lt;p id="caption-attachment-137140" class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 7: Trace detail for gcp-hosted-agent running on GCP&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The telemetry is identical to what an AgentCore runtime-hosted agent produces. Sessions, traces, span metrics, token usage, and latency are all visible in the same dashboard, regardless of where the agent runs.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Although this walkthrough uses Strands Agents, the same ADOT-based pattern applies to other OpenTelemetry-compatible agent frameworks.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;When choosing how to deploy your AI agents, understanding the observability trade-offs between different runtime environments helps you make the right architectural decision. Agents deployed directly on Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime benefit from automatic observability configuration. Agents running in non-AWS environments require additional manual setup but offer greater deployment flexibility. The following comparison highlights the key differences in telemetry collection, credential management, and use cases to help you determine the best approach for your requirements.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Aspect&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Non-AWS Runtime&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;AgentCore runtime&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Telemetry supported&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;ADOT – manual OTEL variables required&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;ADOT – Automatic in-built OTEL variables&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Credential management&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;IAM access key/secret or IAM Roles Anywhere&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Automatic (IAM role)&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Best for&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Agents on-premises, GCP, Azure, or a non-AWS environment&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Agents deployed on AWS with AgentCore&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="validated-environments"&gt;Validated environments&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;We tested the ADOT auto-instrumentation approach across two non-AWS environments:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt; 
 &lt;tbody&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Environment&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Platform&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Result&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;On-premises (simulated)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Standalone server running in non-AWS environment&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Strands agent reporting telemetry (sessions, traces, spans) in AgentCore Observability&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
  &lt;tr&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Google Cloud Shell (GCP)&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Browser-based terminal running on Google Cloud Platform&lt;/td&gt; 
   &lt;td&gt;Strands agent reporting telemetry (sessions, traces, spans) in AgentCore Observability&lt;/td&gt; 
  &lt;/tr&gt; 
 &lt;/tbody&gt; 
&lt;/table&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="best-practices"&gt;Best practices&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Based on our testing, we recommend the following when setting up cross-platform AgentCore Observability:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;ol type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use consistent naming&lt;/strong&gt;: The &lt;code&gt;service.name&lt;/code&gt; in &lt;code&gt;OTEL_RESOURCE_ATTRIBUTES&lt;/code&gt; becomes the agent name on the dashboard. Use descriptive names that identify the environment (for example, &lt;code&gt;prod-onprem-support-agent&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;staging-gcp-research-agent&lt;/code&gt;).&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Verify with &lt;code&gt;get-caller-identity&lt;/code&gt; first&lt;/strong&gt;: Before running the agent, confirm that your credentials work by running &lt;code&gt;python -c "import boto3; print(boto3.client('sts').get_caller_identity())"&lt;/code&gt;. If this fails, the ADOT also fails silently.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Use Python 3.10 or later&lt;/strong&gt;: The ADOT requires Python 3.10 or later. We recommend Python 3.12 for the best compatibility with all dependencies.&lt;/li&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Set session IDs for multi-turn conversations&lt;/strong&gt;: Use the OpenTelemetry baggage API to propagate session IDs:&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-python"&gt;from opentelemetry import baggage
from opentelemetry.context import attach
ctx = baggage.set_baggage("session.id", "my-session-123")
attach(ctx)&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;ol start="5" type="1"&gt; 
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Rotate credentials regularly&lt;/strong&gt;: For production deployments, avoid long-lived access keys. Consider &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/rolesanywhere/latest/userguide/introduction.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;IAM Roles Anywhere&lt;/a&gt; for on-premises workloads, or use your cloud provider’s identity federation to assume AWS IAM roles.&lt;/li&gt; 
&lt;/ol&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To remove the resources created during this walkthrough:&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt; 
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;# Delete the IAM access key (if created for testing)
aws iam delete-access-key --user-name &amp;lt;your-user&amp;gt; --access-key-id &amp;lt;your-key-id&amp;gt;
# Optionally delete the auto-created CloudWatch log groups
aws logs delete-log-group --log-group-name /aws/bedrock-agentcore/runtimes/my-external-agent --region us-east-1
aws logs delete-log-group --log-group-name /aws/bedrock-agentcore/runtimes/gcp-hosted-agent --region us-east-1&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt; 
&lt;/div&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;This walkthrough uses Amazon Bedrock, Amazon CloudWatch, and AWS X-Ray, which incur costs. See the respective pricing pages for details.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore Observability isn’t limited to agents running on AgentCore runtime or within AWS. Using ADOT auto-instrumentation with IAM credentials and the correct OpenTelemetry environment variables, you can send telemetry from your choice of environment with internet access. Your agents can run on-premises, on GCP, on Azure, or anywhere else and still report to the same AgentCore Observability dashboard.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;The setup requires a &lt;code&gt;pip install&lt;/code&gt; and a set of environment variables. The resulting telemetry is identical to what AgentCore runtime-hosted agents produce: sessions, traces, span metrics, and token usage, all in one unified view.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;To get started, clone the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-external-agents-telemetry-on-agentcore-observability" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;sample code from GitHub&lt;/a&gt; and follow the instructions in the README to configure and run the agent in your environment.&lt;/p&gt; 
&lt;p&gt;For agents already running on AWS but outside AgentCore runtime (EKS, ECS, Lambda), refer to the &lt;a href="https://github.com/awslabs/agentcore-samples/tree/main/03-integrations/agents-hosted-outside-runtime" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AgentCore Observability for EKS-hosted agents&lt;/a&gt; tutorial. For agents on AgentCore runtime, observability is configured automatically. See &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/observability-configure.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Add observability to your AgentCore resources&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
